ܘܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܡܬܡܠܠ ܗܘܐ ܫܪ̈ܒܐ ܒܝܢܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܡܬܬܢܐ ܗܘܬ ܩܝܡܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܢ ܢܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܩܕܡ ܨܠܘ̈ܒܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܬܦܠܓܝܢ ܥܠ ܡܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܒܗܿܝ ܕܚܙܘ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܩܒܠܘ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܕܢܕܓܠܘܢ ܘܡܕܡ ܚܠܦ ܡܕܡ ܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܠܥܡܐ ܟܕ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܗܿܝ ܕܗܘܬ ܡܬܡܠܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܝܢܬܗܘܢ ܀
20 Thus every time the story was told from one side and the other the resurrection of Christ was repeated by the guards before those who had crucified him, without anyone questioning their words about what they had seen, because they had taken it upon themselves to lie and to say one thing in place of the other to the people, while secretly what had taken place was evoked among them. The resurrection is not announced by the friends but by the enemies of Christ.
ܘܡܢ ܗܘ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܟܠ ܝܘܡ ܒܝܢܬܗܘܢ ܡܬܡܠܠ ܗܘܐ. ܟܠܝܘܡ ܢܘܚܡܗ ܕܡܪܢ ܩܕܡܝܗܘܢ ܠܒܗܬܬܗܘܢ ܡܬܬܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܘ ܡܢ ܫܡܥܘܢ ܘܝܘܚܢܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܩܣܛܘܢܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܦ ܛܠܡܘ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܗܝܘܢ ܓܢܒܘܗܝ ܘܠܘ ܡܬܢܚܡܘ ܐܬܢܚܡ. ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܣܗ̈ܕܐ ܠܢܘܚܡܗ ܠܐ ܡܫܕܪ ܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܨܝܕ ܒܥܠܕܒܒ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܢ ܒܥܠܕܒܒܘܬܐ ܢܬܒܣܪ̈ܢ ܡܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܡܣܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܒܐ̈ܦܐ ܫܩܠܝܢ.
21 But what mystery was every day evoked among them? Every day the resurrection of Our Lord was repeated before them (the Jews) to their shame, not by Simon and by John, his friends, but by the soldiers who were also slandering the disciples by saying that it was they who had stolen him and that he had absolutely not risen. To be witnesses of his resurrection, he had not sent his friends to his enemies, so that their


[p. 640] {#po.43.p640}
ܩܝܦܐ ܠܟܐܦܐ ܠܐ ܫܡܥ ܗܘܐ. ܚܢܢ ܠܝܘܚܢܢ ܠܐ ܡܬܛܦܝܣ ܗܘܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗܢܐ ܠܩܘܣܛܘܢܪ̈ܐ ܚܘܝ ܗܘܐ ܩܝܡܬܗ ܕܗܘܘ ܡܡܠܠܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܙܩܦܘܗܝ ܩܕܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܫܠܡܘܗܝ ܠܛܟܣܗ ܕܦܝܠܛܣ. ܘܠܓܘ̈ܕܐ ܕܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܩܣܛܘܢܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܘ ܫܠܝ̈ܚܐ ܥܠ ܩܝܡܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܘܕܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܡܬܩܒܠܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܥܠ ܕܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܘ ܕܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܘܠܘ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܗܘܘ ܕܙܩܝܦܐ ܀ ܘܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܘܗܝ ܕܡܪܢ ܟܢܝܫܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܨܝܕܝܗܘܢ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܕܢܐܠܦܘܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܡܢ ܚܙܘ ܨܝܕ ܩܒܪܗ ܕܩܛܝܠܐ. ܘܬܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܚܙܘ ܟܕ ܡܙܗܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܢܫܡܥ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܢܛܪܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܪܐܙܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܪܓܫܘܢ ܒܗ ܒܪ̈ܝܐ. ܘܒܝܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܫܬܟܚܬ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܓܠܝܐ ܘܢܗܝܪܐ ܠܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܘܠܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܘܐܝܕܝܥ ܘܫܪܝܪ ܢܘܚܡܐ ܠܙܩ̈ܘܦܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܢܚܕܘܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܘܢܬܬܒܪ ܠܒܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܀ ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܥܢܢܐ ܕܥܩ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܢܬܡܠܝ ܒܙܪ̈ܝܦܘܬܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܝܗ̇ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܡܥܪܩܐ ܗܘܬ. ܝܘܡܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܙܩ̈ܘܦܐ ܒܡ̈ܫܒܝܗܿ ܘܒܟܝ̈ܡܘܢܝܗܿ ܩܫܝܐܝܬ ܚܒܛܐ ܗܘܬ. ܥܕܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܫܬܚܠܦܘ܀ ܕܝܘܡܢܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܥܠ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܨܚܘܐ ܦܨܝܚܐ ܕܟܠ ܚܕܘ̈ܢ ܘܥܠ ܨܠܘ̈ܒܐ ܣܬܘܐ ܟܡܝܪܐ ܕܡܠܐ ܥܩ̈ܬܐ. ܘܗܝ ܓܝܓܠܐ ܕܒܥܪܘܒܬܐ ܒܕܡܐ ܛܒܝܥܐ ܗܘܬ ܕܬܚܬ ܩܛܝܠܐ ܠܓܘ ܫܝܘܠ. ܗܐ ܝܘܡܢܐ ܟܕܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܡܟܪܟ ܠܗܿ ܢܘܚܡܐ ܕܬܣܩ ܚܝܐ ܡܢ ܩܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܀
words would not be suspected of enmity by people suspicious of their bias. Caiaphas did not listen to Cephas. Annas was not persuaded by John. That is why he (Christ) had shown his resurrection to the soldiers because those who had crucified him spoke of it with trembling in the presence of those who had delivered him to Pilate's troop. For all those who had crucified him, the soldiers were the apostles of the resurrection of Christ and they were easily accepted, for they were close to those who had crucified him and not to the friends of the crucified. 22 All the enemies of Our Lord had gathered at their place in secret, to learn from them what they had seen near the tomb of the dead man. And in secret they recounted how they had seen, while distrusting one another, so that no one would hear, except those who kept the secret that strangers were not to sense. It is for these reasons that the resurrection was manifest and clear to the enemies as to the friends, the survival evident and sure for those who had crucified as for the disciples, so that the friends might rejoice and the heart of the enemies be broken. The brilliant light of the resurrection cannot be hidden. 23 The cloud of catastrophes which, the day before yesterday, by a storm of sufferings had put the disciples to flight, today has fallen violently upon those who crucified him with its storms and hurricanes. The seasons are reversed, to the point that today there is a resplendent spring full of joys for the disciples, while there is a black winter full of catastrophes for those who crucified him. The wheel which on Friday was immersed in blood to lower the dead man into Sheol, today life has attached itself to it and the resurrection makes it turn so that it brings the living man back up from the tomb to heaven.
ܠܐܝܘ ܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܕܢܚܦܘܢ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܟܚܘ. ܝܗܒܘ ܫܘܚܕܐ ܕܢܩܝܡܘܢ ܕܓܠܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܩܡܬ. ܕܗܒܗܘܢ ܐܘܒܕܘ ܘܩܘܫܬܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܚܦܝ. ܠܣܛܝ̈ܐ ܠܠܐ ܒܥܘ ܕܢܓܢܒܘܢ ܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܢܛܫܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܐܣܬܟܠܘ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܡܣܬܪܐ ܠܗ. ܓܢ̈ܒܐ ܣܟܠܐ ܣܒܪܘ ܕܢܣܝܡܘܢ ܫܡܫܐ ܒܥܘܒܗܘܢ ܘܢܥܪܩܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܢܬܠܘܢ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܡܬܦܪ̈ܢܣܐ ܒܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ. ܐܝܡܡܐ ܕܢܚ ܥܠ ܣܘ̈ܦܐ ܘܥܘܝܪ̈ܐ ܐܓܪ̈ܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܦܥ̈ܠܐ ܕܢܦܩܘܢ ܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܢܓܗܬ ܀ ܕܢܚܬ ܩܝܡܬܗ ܕܒܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܬܦܨܚܘ ܒܗܿ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܥܠܝ̈ܐ ܘܥܘ̈ܡܩܐ ܬܚܬܝ̈ܐ. ܘܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܝܗܒܘ ܫܘܚܕܐ ܕܢܦܩܘܢ ܛܒ̈ܐ ܘܥܘ̈ܡܩܐ ܬܚܬܝ̈ܐ. ܘܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܝܗܒܘ ܫܘܚܕܐ ܕܢܦܩܘܢ ܛܒ̈ܐ
24 Those who had crucified him exhausted themselves trying to obscure the truth, but without succeeding. They offered a bribe to organize a lie, but it did not stand up. They lost their gold and the truth was not hidden. Senseless brigands tried to steal and hide the great day without understanding that they had no place to conceal it. Stupid thieves believed (they could) place the sun in their bosom, flee and deprive creation of being administered by its light. The great day shone to the ends (of the earth) and blind men hired mercenaries so that they would go and tell creation that it would rise no more. 25 The resurrection of the Son shone and the (most) elevated heights as well as the (most) profound abysses rejoiced in it. But those who


[p. 642] {#po.43.p642}
ܕܓ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܬܓܢܒܘ ܐܬܓܢܒ ܘܠܘ ܡܬܢܚܡܘ ܐܬܢܚܡ. ܒܦܠܓܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܐܓܪܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܣܠܝܩ ܫܡܫܐ. ܘܟܕ ܡܬܦܪܣܝܢ ܕܢܚܦܘܢ ܠܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܐܬܦܪ̈ܣܝܘ ܕܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ ܗܝ ܕܓܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܠܝܬ ܫܡܥܐ ܠܡܠܬܗܘܢ. ܥܕ ܓܝܪ ܒܥܝܢ ܕܢܐܡܪܘܢ ܕܡܬܓܢܒܘ ܐܬܓܢܒ ܐܬܚܘܝ ܠܗ̇ ܠܡܪܝܡ. ܐܦܓܥ ܒܗ ܒܡܪ̈ܬܐ. ܚܘܝ ܠܗ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ. ܗܠܟ ܠܗ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܥܡ ܩܠܝܘܦܐ ܘܐܚܘܗܝ. ܚܒܨܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܛܒ̈ܐ ܠܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܕܟܘܠ ܕܚܙܝܗܝ ܪܗܝܒ ܕܢܣܒܪ ܠܟܢܬܗ.
crucified him offered a bribe so that false news would spread, namely that he had really been stolen and that he had not risen at all. In broad daylight, they took men into their pay to say that the sun had not risen. And as they plotted to obscure the light, they were unmasked because their lie was vain and no one listened to their discourse. Indeed, while they were trying to say that he had really been stolen, he appeared to Mary, he came to meet Martha, he showed himself to the disciples and he walked with Cleopas and his brother. The news crossed because each of those who had seen him hastened to announce it to his companion. The session of the angels at the tomb is the irrefutable testimony of the
ܝܬܒܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܠܐ̈ܟܐ ܥܠ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܩܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܟܘܠ ܡܢ ܕܐܬܐ ܢܣܒܪܘܢ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܬܐ. ܪ̈ܒܐ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܘܚܢܩܗܿ ܠܕܓܠܘܬܐ. ܣܓܝ ܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܣܝܒܪܗ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܠܡܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܝܬܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܡܠܐ̈ܟܐ ܒܝܬ ܩܒܘܪܐ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܣܒܪܘܢ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܥܠ ܩܝܡܬܗ. ܐܬܐܡܪ ܓܝܪ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܠܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܬܐ ܕܝܕܥܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܠܝܫܘܥ ܕܐܙܕܩܦ ܒܥܝܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ. ܠܝܬ ܗܘ ܬܢܢ ܩܡ ܠܗ. ܘܬܘܒ ܕܠܡܢ ܒܥܝܢ ܐܢܬܝܢ ܚܝܐ ܥܡ ܡܝ̈ܬܐ. ܝܘܡܢ ܐܬܚܙܝܘ ܡܠܐ̈ܟܐ ܨܝܕ ܩܒܪܐ. ܡܢܬܡ ܕܝܢ ܡܛܠ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܐܬܚܙܝܘ ܨܝܕ ܓܓܘܠܬܐ. ܐܒܐ ܐܫܠܡ ܠܒܪܗ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܕܒܚܐ ܘܟܠܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܥ̈ܒܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܩܪܒܘܢ ܨܝܕ ܙܩܝܦܐ ܕܠܐ ܟܕ ܚܙܝܢ ܠܡܪ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܡܨܛܥܪ ܢܬܓܘܙܠ ܛܢܢܗܘܢ ܘܢܣܦܘܢ ܢܘܪܐ ܒܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܘܢܦܘܫ ܠܗ ܙܩܝܦܐ ܡܢ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܕܚ̈ܫܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܝܐ ܠܗ.
resurrection of Our Lord. 26 The angels sat at the door of the tomb in order to announce what was true to all those who would come. The truth grew and suffocated the lie. The light abounded and the darkness did not endure it. But why then were the angels sitting in the tomb, if not to announce his resurrection to the disciples? Indeed they said to the women, his disciples: We know that you are looking for Jesus, the crucified. He is not here, he is risen and again: Why do you look for the Living among the dead? 27 Today the angels appeared at the tomb, but the day before yesterday why did they not appear at Golgotha? The Father delivered his Son so that he would be the victim, but he prevented his servants from approaching the cross, so that by seeing their Lord suffering the outrage, they would not be inflamed with wrath, that they would not strike down those who were crucifying (him) and that the crucified one would not turn away from the path of the passion that was imposed on him.
ܘܐܢ ܟܝܐ ܒܣܕܘܡ ܥܠ ܕܐܨܛܥܪ ܠܘܛ ܫܦܘ ܐܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܘܐܬܪ̈ܘ ܓܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܘܩܕܘܗܿ. ܒܐܘܪ̈ܫܠܡ ܩܛܠܬ ܡܪܗܿ ܐܠܘ ܚܙܘ ܠܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܬܒܙܚ ܐܝܟܢ ܡܣܝܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܠܐ ܢܚܘܘܢ ܛܢܢܗܘܢ ܒܓܙܪ ܕܝܢܝܐ ܚܪ̈ܝܦܐ. ܐܬܓܡܪܬ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܕܨܥܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܦܩܕ ܐܒܐ ܠܥ̈ܒܕܐ ܕܢܚܬܘܢ ܠܐܝܩܪܐ ܕܒܪܗ.
28 And if, at Sodom, because Lot was outraged, they gently shook their wings, made burning coals fall and set it on fire, how at Jerusalem, which killed its Lord, if they had seen the Son who was being outraged, could they have endured not showing their wrath by a prompt punishment? (But) the path of his outrages was followed to the end and then the Father ordered his servants to descend to glorify his Son.
ܢܦܩ ܡܪܢ ܡܢ ܩܒܪܐ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܦܬܚܘܗܝ ܡܠܐ̈ܟܐ. ܠܘ ܡܛܘܠ ܕܢܦܘܩ ܗܘ ܦܬܚܘܗܝ ܡܠܐ̈ܟܐ. ܐܠܐ ܕܢܚܙܘܢ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘ
29 Our Lord came out of the tomb and then the angels opened it. It was not for him to come out that the angels opened it, [but they were


[p. 644] {#po.43.p644}
ܒܓܘ ܩܒܪܐ ܐܝܨܦܘ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ. ܟܬܝܒ ܓܝܪ ܕܡܠܐܟܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܥܓܠ ܟܐܦܐ ܗܝ ܘܝܬܒ ܗܘܐ ܥܠܝܗ̇.
anxious that the disciples see that he was no longer inside. Indeed, it is written that the angel of the Lord rolled back the stone and that he had sat on it
ܐܠܘ ܒܓܘ ܩܒܪܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܪܢ ܠܐ ܝܬܒ ܡܠܐܟܐ. ܡܚܕܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܥܓܠܗ̇ ܟܬܝܒ ܕܝܬܒ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܘܝܕܝܥܐ ܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܡܪܚ ܗܘܐ ܥܒܕܐ ܕܢܬܒ ܩܕܡ ܡܪܗ ܕܢܚܘܐ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܬܝܢ ܕܢܦܝܩ ܗܘ ܡܪܢ ܡܢ ܩܒܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܦܠܓܘܢ ܥܠ ܢܘܚܡܗ ܟܕ ܡܫܟܚܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܐܚܝܕ ܩܒܪܐ ܘܩܝܡܝܢ ܚܬܡܘ̈ܗܝ.
30 If Our Lord had (still) been in the tomb, the angel would not have sat down. (But) immediately after having rolled back the stone, it is written that he sat on it—it is well known that a servant does not dare to remain seated in the presence of his master—to show those who were coming that Our Lord had come out of the tomb, so that they would not doubt his resurrection by finding the tomb closed and the seals intact.
ܒܢܝ̈ܗ̇ ܓܝܪ ܕܩܝܡܬܐ ܠܘ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܢܦܩܝܢ ܐܠܐ ܒܟܝ̈ܢܐ ܛܡܝ̈ܡܐ ܥܒܪ̈ܝܢ. ܢܦܩ ܡܪܢ ܡܢ ܩܒܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܥܠ ܠܥܠܝܬܐ. ܐܠܨܬ ܓܝܪ ܕܢܥܓܠ ܟܐܦܐ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܡܢ ܕܫܪܝܘ ܨܝܒܝܢ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܘܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܬܐ ܕܣܢܝܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܢܚܙܘܢ ܓܘܗ ܕܩܒܪܐ ܘܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܫܠܕܐ ܘܟ̈ܬܢܐ ܕܩܒܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܚܬܝ̈ܬܬܐ ܢܫܬܪ̈ܪ̈ܘܢ ܒܩܝܡܬܐ.
31 For the resurrected do not come out through the doors, but pass through solid substances. (Thus) Our Lord came out of the tomb as he entered the upper room. For it was necessary that the angel roll back the stone after the disciples, men and women, had begun to arrive, because they needed to see the interior of the tomb as well as the location of the corpse and the burial cloths, in order to be assured of the resurrection by all (these) proofs. Conclusion: The praise of the disciples consequence of the evidence of the resurrection
ܘܡܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܓܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܫܡܫܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܢ ܪܘܙܘ ܬܠܡܝ̈ܕܐ ܒܢܘܚܡܐ ܘܐܬܟܡܪ̈ܘ ܙܩܘ̈ܦܐ ܒܩܝܡܬܐ. ܘܟܕ ܓܠܝ̈ܢ ܐܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܫܠܝ̈ܚܐ ܐܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܝܕܥܝܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܩܡ ܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܘܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܡܐܬ ܘܡܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܠܛ ܒܗ. ܕܠܗ ܬܫ̈ܒܚܢ ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ. ܐܡܝܢ.
of Our Lord. 32 Thus, because of all these facts which were clear and brilliant as the sun, the disciples exulted because of the resurrection and those who had crucified him were afflicted because of it. That is why the apostles say with assurance: We know that Christ has risen from the abode of the dead, that he dies no more and that death has no more power over him. To him be the praises for the age of ages. Amen.
